## The Rule

**Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action** — including clarifying questions, exploring the codebase, or checking files. If it turns out wrong for the situation, you don't have to use it.

**Before entering plan mode:** if you haven't already brainstormed, invoke the brainstorming skill first.

Then announce "Using [skill] to [purpose]" and follow the skill exactly. If it has a checklist, create a todo per item.

## Skill Priority

When multiple skills apply, process skills come first — they set the approach, then implementation skills (frontend-design, etc.) carry it out. Brainstorming and systematic-debugging are Superpowers' most common process skills, but the rule holds for any of them.

- "Let's build X" → superpowers:brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
- "Fix this bug" → superpowers:systematic-debugging first, then domain skills.

## Red Flags

These thoughts mean STOP—you're rationalizing:

| Thought | Reality |
|---------|---------|
| "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
| "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
| "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
| "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
| "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
| "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
| "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
| "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
| "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
| "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
| "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
| "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept ≠ using the skill. Invoke it. |

---
name: multi-model-verify
description: Use when planning a reference port, an API-sensitive module, or any change risky enough to need cross-model verification before implementation, or when an implemented diff must be checked against its plan and reference before merge. Fires automatically alongside superpowers requesting-code-review via the review-companion hook.
---

# Multi-Model Verify

## Overview

Two equal-weight advisors — Fable 5 (this session) and GPT-5.6 Sol (via the
codex CLI) — verify and refute each other's claims before the cheap
implementer touches code. Sol's documented fabrication risk (METR) is
mitigated by the debate structure — evidence grounding plus mutual
refutation — not by down-weighting either side.

**REQUIRED READING before the first round:** references/debate-protocol.md.

Companion to superpowers, not a replacement: it fills the cross-model review
gap superpowers rules out of scope. `/codex:adversarial-review` remains the
human-invoked pre-merge gate; if the codex plugin's stop-review-gate is
toggled on, its stop-time review overlaps mode `diff` — expected, not a bug.

## When to use

- **Mode `plan`** — after superpowers brainstorming proposes approaches for a
  port or API-sensitive module, before the implementation plan is written.
- **Mode `diff`** — after implementation, alongside superpowers
  requesting-code-review (the review-companion hook injects this
  automatically with the matching base/head SHAs).
- NOT for plain bugfixes, docs or release chores, GUI tweaks, or lint sweeps.

## Preflight (both modes)

1. `codex --version` must succeed — on failure follow references/fallbacks.md
   (degraded mode, visibly flagged; never silently skip cross-vendor review).
2. For port work: the reference source must exist under `References/`
   (quote paths — some contain spaces, e.g. `References/M+ Timer`). Missing
   → **HARD STOP before any debate round runs**: ask the user for the path
   and go no further — do not run a codex exchange about the absence, do
   not debate from memory, do not start a degraded mode. If the folder
   holds version subdirectories (e.g. a `v1.4/` next to a `v1.1_old/`), use
   the newest unless the user names one, and cite the versioned path:
   `References/<name>/<version>/<file>:<line>`.
   For non-port work there is no reference folder — claims ground in the
   project's own source, specs, or upstream docs instead, same strike rule.

## Mode plan

1. Draft the Fable position: chosen approach, port-fidelity claims, and the
   API/behavior risk register. Every reference claim cites a
   `References/<name>/<file>:<line>` actually read this session — anchor
   EVERY file with its full path the first time you cite it, manifests and
   secondary files included (shorthand only once that file is anchored);
   every API claim cites authoritative local docs (WoW projects:
   `.wow-api-reference/` or a dated in-game probe result) — never memory.
   The position has no separate artifact — it IS the claims section of the
   step-2 brief.
2. Compose Sol's debate brief per references/model-prompting-notes.md, write
   it to a scratchpad file, then run round 1:

   ```powershell
   Get-Content -Raw <brief-file> | codex exec --sandbox read-only -m <canonical-model-id> -c model_reasoning_effort=<canonical-effort> --output-last-message <reply-file> -
   ```

   `<canonical-model-id>` and `<canonical-effort>` are the two declarations
   in references/model-prompting-notes.md — read the literal values from
   there, never from memory (the reviewer swaps by editing that one file,
   and a remembered id silently defeats the swap).

   Capture the `session id:` line printed in the run header (top of output);
   read Sol's reply from `<reply-file>` — the stdout transcript logs every
   file Sol reads and can run tens of KB, with the reply buried at the
   bottom.
3. Later rounds keep Sol's state by resuming that session — flags MUST
   precede the resume subcommand (flags after it are a usage error):

   ```powershell
   codex exec --sandbox read-only -m <canonical-model-id> -c model_reasoning_effort=<canonical-effort> --output-last-message <reply-file> resume <SESSION_ID> "<rebuttal-brief>"
   ```

4. Iterate per debate-protocol.md until convergence or the round cap, then
   escalate any unresolved points to the user with both positions stated.
5. Freeze the converged plan per references/frozen-plan-format.md under the
   project's superpowers plans dir (KitnEssentials:
   `dev/docs/superpowers/plans/`; other projects: the superpowers default
   `docs/superpowers/plans/`).

## Mode diff

Same transport and protocol. First read the frozen plan's debate record and
its **Verification status** field. Then the brief carries the frozen plan
path, the base/head SHAs superpowers code review used, and the
`git diff base..head` output. Both sides check **spec fidelity** (drift from
the frozen plan — the implementer makes zero judgment calls, so any drift is
a finding) and, for port work, **port fidelity** (drift from the reference
source), ending PASS / FIX / ESCALATE.

**Degraded-plan poisoning rule:** a plan whose Verification status is
DEGRADED cannot produce an ordinary diff PASS. Mode diff must first reopen
and cross-verify the plan's claims and approved deviations (the
retrospective pass the plan never got), and only then verify the
implementation against it — confirming a diff matches an unsound plan
verifies nothing. If cross-vendor verification is STILL unavailable, the
only terminal state is `ESCALATE — CROSS-VENDOR GATE UNSATISFIED`: merging
then requires the user's explicitly recorded risk acceptance, never a PASS.

## Finish line

Before it: the session's final-adjudication step (debate-protocol.md) —
verify the last round's findings against the repo and issue the terminal
verdict; a reviewer's PASS/FIX is never terminal by itself.

End with one status line: participating models, rounds used, converged vs
escalated points, and the verification status — `FULL`, or
`DEGRADED (<class>, authorized by user at round N)` per fallbacks.md. The
session emits this line itself; never delegate it to a subagent.

## Common mistakes

- Accepting Sol's claims about reference code without the cited lines —
  strike the claim per protocol; do not argue against it.
- Re-sending the full debate context each round instead of resuming the
  codex session.
- Running mode `diff` against different SHAs than the code review used.
- Treating convergence as failure — a sound plan converging in one round is
  the system working, not a skipped debate.

## Subagent dispatch requires multi-agent support

Add to your Codex config (`~/.codex/config.toml`):

```toml
[features]
multi_agent = true
```

This enables `spawn_agent`, `wait_agent`, and `close_agent` for skills like `dispatching-parallel-agents` and `subagent-driven-development`. When using subagent-driven-development, close reviewer subagents when their review returns. Keep each implementer subagent open until its task's review passes — the fix loop resumes the implementer — then close it. If your harness cannot send another message to a spawned agent, dispatch each fix round as a fresh implementer carrying the brief, the report file, and the findings.

## Environment Detection

Skills that create worktrees or finish branches should detect their
environment with read-only git commands before proceeding:

```bash
GIT_DIR=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
GIT_COMMON=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
BRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)
```

- `GIT_DIR != GIT_COMMON` → already in a linked worktree (skip creation)
- `BRANCH` empty → detached HEAD (cannot branch/push/PR from sandbox)

See `using-git-worktrees` Step 0 and `finishing-a-development-branch`
Step 1 for how each skill uses these signals.

## Codex App Finishing

When the sandbox blocks branch/push operations (detached HEAD in an
externally managed worktree), the agent commits all work and informs
the user to use the App's native controls:

- **"Create branch"** — names the branch, then commit/push/PR via App UI
- **"Hand off to local"** — transfers work to the user's local checkout

The agent can still run tests, stage files, and output suggested branch
names, commit messages, and PR descriptions for the user to copy.

# Debate protocol

Both advisors are equal weight. Neither model's claim outranks the other's;
only evidence does.

## Evidence grounding (the strike rule)

- **Every externally checkable claim must carry a citation to a source the
  other side can read**: a file:line in the repo or reference material the
  citing side actually read (Fable: read this session; Sol: read inside its
  sandboxed run — its quoted excerpt counts as reading), authoritative local
  docs, or a fetched URL.
- Source-specific examples: reference-port claims cite
  `References/<name>/<file>:<line>`; WoW 12.0 API claims cite
  `.wow-api-reference/` or a dated in-game probe result; library or platform
  claims cite the vendored source or official docs.
- **Anchor every file the first time you cite it** — full repo-relative path
  with line, `References/DemoWidget/DemoWidget.toc:1`, not `DemoWidget.toc:1`.
  This holds for EVERY file, including one-line manifests and secondary
  files, not just the main source. Once a file is anchored, shorthand
  (`Core.lua:19`) is fine for later citations of that same file. A bare
  filename does not locate anything for the other side: the project has its
  own `DemoAddon.toc` and the reference has `DemoWidget.toc`, and an
  unanchored `.toc:1` could be either.
- A claim with no citation is **struck, not debated**. Note it as struck and
  move on — arguing against an ungrounded claim launders it into the record.
- Reports are claims requiring evidence. "The reference handles this" without
  the file:line is an assertion, not a finding.

## Round structure

Each exchange (one Fable position or rebuttal + one Sol reply) is a round.

1. From round 2 on, state position changes since last round: which of the
   other side's points are **accepted** (absorbed into the plan), which are
   **refuted** (with the evidence that refutes them), which are **struck**
   (no citation).
2. Raise new risks only with evidence attached.
3. End with a verdict on the current plan/diff:
   - **PASS** — no remaining substantive objections.
   - **FIX** — named change required, with the specific fix and its evidence.
   - **ESCALATE** — a disagreement evidence cannot settle (taste, missing
     in-game data, ambiguous reference intent).

## Convergence and the round cap

- Converged: both sides verdict PASS in the same round.
- **Converged with amendments**: the final round's only outstanding verdicts
  are FIXes whose fixes the other side accepts on the record. Record each
  accepted fix in the debate record and treat the plan as converged — an
  accepted FIX is agreement, not a dispute for the user to settle. (This
  matters whenever a FIX lands in the last round before the cap.)
- Round cap: **4 exchanges** by default (caller may raise or lower it).
  Hitting the cap is not failure — it is the signal to stop spending tokens
  on an argument evidence hasn't settled.
- At the cap or on ESCALATE, for points that remain genuinely contested:
  stop, present BOTH positions to the user with their evidence, and let the
  user decide. Never silently pick a side.
- Questions only runtime testing can answer always escalate — neither model
  can run the live application (in WoW projects, /reload + BugSack is always
  the user's step).

## Final adjudication (the session's last step)

The chain never ends on the external reviewer's verdict. After Sol's final
round — including a re-review of fixes — the SESSION performs one closing
step before anything merges or freezes:

1. Verify each finding from that final round against the live repo (read
   the cited lines; run the gates if implicated) — a final-round finding is
   itself an unverified claim until checked.
2. Accept with evidence, refute with evidence, or ESCALATE to the user.
   Nothing is adopted or dismissed on authority.
3. Emit the terminal verdict and the finish line. A reviewer PASS/FIX is
   input to this step, never the decision itself.

Equal weight governs claims during the debate; adjudication is procedural,
not a rank: the session is the accountable party with live-repo access, so
the session always has the final say — and a genuine deadlock still goes to
the user, never to whoever spoke last (user directive, 2026-07-12).

## Anti-sycophancy, anti-theater

Debates fail two ways: rubber-stamping and manufactured conflict. Both are
defects.

- **A sound plan gets one line.** "Plan is sound; the one thing to watch is
  X." Converging in round 1 is the system working.
- **Do not manufacture objections** to justify the round. If you would not
  push back in a real review, do not push back here.
- Do not restate the other side's point in different words as a "new" risk.
- Do not concede a point you can refute just to converge faster —
  unresolved disagreement has an honest exit (ESCALATE).

## Why the structure carries the trust

GPT-5.6 Sol has a documented (METR, OpenAI system card) tendency to fabricate
results under evaluation pressure. The mitigation is this protocol — every
claim must survive citation and refutation by an independent model — not
down-weighting Sol's verdicts. The same rules bind Fable equally: fabricated
detail is a known failure mode of every frontier model, and this project's
history (see memory: no-fabrication feedback) treats invented API behavior as
the cardinal sin.

name: verification-before-completion
description: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always

# Verification Before Completion

## Overview

**Core principle:** Evidence before claims, always.

**Violating the letter of this rule is violating the spirit of this rule.**

## The Iron Law

```
NO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE
```

If you haven't run the verification command in this message, you cannot claim it passes.

## The Gate Function

```
BEFORE claiming any status or expressing satisfaction:

1. IDENTIFY: What command proves this claim?
2. RUN: Execute the FULL command (fresh, complete)
3. READ: Full output, check exit code, count failures
4. VERIFY: Does output confirm the claim?
   - If NO: State actual status with evidence
   - If YES: State claim WITH evidence
5. ONLY THEN: Make the claim

Skip any step = lying, not verifying
```

## Common Failures

| Claim | Requires | Not Sufficient |
|-------|----------|----------------|
| Tests pass | Test command output: 0 failures | Previous run, "should pass" |
| Linter clean | Linter output: 0 errors | Partial check, extrapolation |
| Build succeeds | Build command: exit 0 | Linter passing, logs look good |
| Bug fixed | Test original symptom: passes | Code changed, assumed fixed |
| Regression test works | Red-green cycle verified | Test passes once |
| Agent completed | VCS diff shows changes | Agent reports "success" |
| Requirements met | Line-by-line checklist | Tests passing |

## Red Flags - STOP

- Using "should", "probably", "seems to"
- Expressing satisfaction before verification ("Great!", "Perfect!", "Done!", etc.)
- About to commit/push/PR without verification
- Trusting agent success reports
- Relying on partial verification
- Thinking "just this once"
- Tired and wanting work over
- **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**

## Rationalization Prevention

| Excuse | Reality |
|--------|---------|
| "Should work now" | RUN the verification |
| "I'm confident" | Confidence ≠ evidence |
| "Just this once" | No exceptions |
| "Linter passed" | Linter ≠ compiler |
| "Agent said success" | Verify independently |
| "I'm tired" | Exhaustion ≠ excuse |
| "Partial check is enough" | Partial proves nothing |
| "Different words so rule doesn't apply" | Spirit over letter |

## Key Patterns

**Tests:**
```
✅ [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] "All tests pass"
❌ "Should pass now" / "Looks correct"
```

**Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**
```
✅ Write → Run (pass) → Revert fix → Run (MUST FAIL) → Restore → Run (pass)
❌ "I've written a regression test" (without red-green verification)
```

**Build:**
```
✅ [Run build] [See: exit 0] "Build passes"
❌ "Linter passed" (linter doesn't check compilation)
```

**Requirements:**
```
✅ Re-read plan → Create checklist → Verify each → Report gaps or completion
❌ "Tests pass, phase complete"
```

**Agent delegation:**
```
✅ Agent reports success → Check VCS diff → Verify changes → Report actual state
❌ Trust agent report
```

## When To Apply

**ALWAYS before:**
- ANY variation of success/completion claims
- ANY expression of satisfaction
- ANY positive statement about work state
- Committing, PR creation, task completion
- Moving to next task
- Delegating to agents

**Rule applies to:**
- Exact phrases
- Paraphrases and synonyms
- Implications of success
- ANY communication suggesting completion/correctness

CLAUDE.md
# parallax — Claude Code plugin (NOT a WoW addon)

This repo is developer tooling: a Claude Code plugin providing cross-model
verification (session ⇄ cross-vendor reviewer debates; the reviewer lane is
declared in the skill's model-prompting-notes.md) plus its eval harness. It lives
under KitnDev for convenience, but the WoW addon family conventions in
`../AGENTS.md` (dev loop, /reload, luacheck/busted, 12.0 API rules,
References/) do NOT apply here — only the git basics do (feature branches
for real work, lowercase imperative commits, no AI attribution).

## Verification
- `python evals/tools/skill_lint.py skills/multi-model-verify --strict`
- `python evals/tools/skill_scanner.py skills`
- `python evals/tools/run_trigger_evals.py`
- `python -m pytest evals -q`
CI runs all four on every push (.github/workflows/skill-evals.yml).

Two suites are local-only and opt-in, so run them by hand when you touch
what they cover:
- skill/prompt changes -> `python evals/tools/run_behavioral_evals.py`
  (real headless runs, graded by the cross-vendor reviewer; `--head` tests
  the checkout instead of the installed cache; for small contract edits
  `--changed` runs only cases whose declared surface intersects the diff
  vs main, printing every skip by name).
- `tools/check-drift.ps1` changes -> run
  `evals/tools/drift_statemachine_tests.ps1` (or, to go through pytest:
  `$env:PARALLAX_STATEMACHINE = "1"; python -m pytest evals -q` — it is
  PowerShell, so no `VAR=1 cmd` prefix). Drives the real script through its
  whole state machine offline against stub CLIs. Slow: four scenarios
  re-run the full pytest suite inside a disposable worktree.

## Dev loop
The plugin is installed user-scope from a LOCAL marketplace pointing at
this working copy, but installs are VERSIONED CACHE COPIES — checkout
edits are NOT live until you: bump `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, run
`claude plugin update parallax@parallax` (qualified name required),
and restart the session when hooks/ or skills/ changed. A restart alone
only reloads the cached version. GitHub remote (Bmwascher/parallax,
private) serves stable installs on other machines.

## Skill editing rules
The multi-model-verify skill's transport commands (codex exec flags, resume
syntax) are LIVE-VERIFIED contracts locked by evals/multi-model-verify/
test_multi_model_verify.py — change the tests first (they encode review
findings), then the skill.

 233: 
 234: **Files:**
 235: - Create: `agents/flash-implementer.md`
 236: - Modify: `agents/implementer.md` (shared-contract markers around its "The contract" section; Lane note pointer + stale-sentence retirement)

 241: 
 242: - [ ] **Step 1: Write `agents/flash-implementer.md` exactly as follows**
 243: 
 244: ````markdown
 245: ---
 246: name: flash-implementer
 247: description: Zero-judgment Flash implementer for frozen-plan tasks. Use when executing build tasks from a debate-frozen implementation plan - give it ONE task's verbatim text plus the plan's Global Constraints and a log-file path. It delegates ALL code-writing to Gemini 3.6 Flash via the Antigravity CLI headlessly, verifies route and authorship evidence, runs the task's verification itself, and reports. It never types repo code and never makes design decisions.
 248: model: haiku
 249: tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash
 250: ---
 251: 
 252: # Flash implementer (agy wrapper)
 253: 
 254: You supervise ONE task from a frozen implementation plan. Gemini 3.6 Flash
 255: does ALL the typing through the Antigravity CLI (`agy`); you do preflight,
 256: dispatch, evidence checks, verification, and honest reporting. You never
 257: edit or create repo files yourself — your tool grant has no Edit or Write,
 258: and using Bash to write repo content is equally forbidden: a changed file
 259: the brain transcript cannot account for fails the task. The one declared
 260: carve-out is the brief file below — the sole transient exception to the
 261: never-write rule, and it never survives to the evidence checks.
 262: 
 263: <!-- shared-contract:start -->
 264: ## The contract
 265: 
 266: - Build exactly what the task says: the files it lists, the code it shows,
 267:   the commands it specifies. Nothing else.
 268: - No improvements, no drive-by refactors, no added error handling, no scope
 269:   adjustments. A deviation is a defect even when it looks better — the diff
 270:   gets checked against the plan afterward, and unexplained drift fails it.
 271: - **INPUT GAP rule:** if the task references a file, interface, value, or
 272:   convention that is not in your brief and not discoverable at the exact
 273:   path the task names, STOP and report the gap. Never invent or guess the
 274:   missing piece.
 275: - Run the task's verification commands yourself and read the output. Never
 276:   claim completion without re-running verification — "should work" means
 277:   the task is not done.
 278: <!-- shared-contract:end -->
 279: 
 280: ## Inputs (from the dispatching controller)
 281: 
 282: - The task's verbatim text and the plan's Global Constraints.
 283: - The workspace directory (this cycle: the main checkout only, with the
 284:   sole live-verification exception — the plan's Task 6 trusted scratch
 285:   repo).
 286: - A log-file path OUTSIDE the workspace (the controller owns it; you never
 287:   place logs in the repo tree).
 288: 
 289: ## Preflight (all five must pass BEFORE dispatch)
 290: 
 291: 1. `agy models` (binary at `$LOCALAPPDATA/agy/bin/agy.exe`) — output must
 292:    contain `gemini-3.6-flash-medium`. Anything else (missing binary,
 293:    sign-out, missing model) is blocked.
 294: 2. `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json` — `trustedWorkspaces` must
 295:    contain the workspace directory. If not: blocked, and the report quotes
 296:    the fix ("run one interactive `agy` session in the workspace and approve
 297:    trust").
 298: 3. The same settings file must carry NO file-writing per-tool allow rule
 299:    at all — any `write_file(` entry, whatever path it names, is blocking.
 300:    A persisted settings allow rule is the durable, call-site-invisible
 301:    bypass class — its absence is the load-bearing permission control; path
 302:    spellings vary, so the rule CLASS is banned rather than path-matched.
 303:    If present: blocked, quoting the rule.
 304: 4. `git status --porcelain` in the workspace must be EMPTY. A dirty tree
 305:    makes authorship attribution impossible — blocked, quoting the paths.
 306: 5. No file matching `AGY-TASK-BRIEF-*` exists in the workspace — a stale
 307:    brief means an earlier dispatch died mid-cleanup: blocked.
 308: 
 309: ## Dispatch
 310: 
 311: 1. Write the brief to `<workspace>/AGY-TASK-BRIEF-<unique>.md` with a Bash
 312:    heredoc — `<unique>` is the dispatch log file's basename, so briefs
 313:    never collide. Content: the task's verbatim text, the Global
 314:    Constraints, and the exact files list. (stdin does not reach the model
 315:    in print mode — probed 2026-07-25; the workspace brief file is the
 316:    delivery mechanism.) This file is the sole transient exception to your
 317:    never-write rule.
 318: 2. Run (single line):
 319:    `agy -p "Read the file AGY-TASK-BRIEF-<unique>.md in the workspace and execute it exactly." --model gemini-3.6-flash-medium --add-dir <workspace> --log-file <log-path>`
 320: 3. Delete the brief file immediately after agy exits — on success,
 321:    failure, and interruption alike — and always BEFORE any evidence
 322:    check, so it never appears in `git status`. If your run is resumed
 323:    after an interruption, delete any leftover brief FIRST.
 324: 
 325: ## Route and authorship checks (every run)
 326: 
 327: - On the log file: `Print mode: starting` line present containing
 328:   `model="gemini-3.6-flash-medium"`.
 329: - On the log file: `Propagating selected model override` line present
 330:   (presence only — its display label is not matched).
 331: - Transcript/tree corroboration: parse `conversationID="<uuid>"` from the
 332:   log's `Print mode: starting` line, then read the brain transcript at
 333:   `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/brain/<conversationID>/.system_generated/logs/transcript_full.jsonl`
 334:   (the `--log-file` log itself carries NO file actions — probed). Every
 335:   path git status reports changed must appear in the brain transcript as
 336:   a successful file-changing action. A changed file the transcript never
 337:   mentions means someone other than Flash typed it — blocked, no matter
 338:   what the tests say. A missing transcript is blocked.
 339: - This evidence is client-side: report the route as **requested and
 340:   propagated**, never "used and confirmed". Server-side substitution is
 341:   not detectable from this evidence class.
 342: 
 343: ## Failure handling — loud, never silent
 344: 
 345: Blocked (quote the exact output) on: any preflight failure, the print-mode
 346: soft-deny line ("auto-denied"), nonzero exit, a missing or mismatched
 347: route line, a corroboration mismatch, or writes diverted to agy's internal
 348: scratch (expected files absent from the tree). Never retry with
 349: `--dangerously-skip-permissions` — that flag is forbidden in this lane, as
 350: is ANY approval-bypass flag or persisted per-tool allow rule added to agy
 351: settings. Never complete the work yourself: rerouting a blocked task to a
 352: Claude tier is the user's decision, recorded in the plan's Escalated
 353: points — not yours.
 354: 
 355: ## Report format (your final message)
 356: 
 357: - **STATUS:** done | blocked | INPUT GAP: <exactly what is missing>
 358: - **ROUTE:** the resolved model ID as requested and propagated, plus the
 359:   retained log file's path AND the brain transcript's path
 360: - **FILES CHANGED:** actual paths from `git status` — on blocked, STILL
 361:   list every path Flash already touched so the session can revert a
 362:   partial write
 363: - **VERIFICATION:** each command you ran yourself, with its real output
 364:   (condensed)
 365: - **DEVIATIONS:** must be "none" — anything else means you stopped and are
 366:   explaining why the task could not be built as written
 367: 
 368: ## Lane note
 369: 
 370: This agent pins the Flash implementation lane. Canonical model literal:
 371: `gemini-3.6-flash-medium` (Gemini 3.6 Flash, medium reasoning effort,
 372: Antigravity CLI resolved ID). The literal lives ONLY here;
 373: `implementer.md` pins its own lane's model in its frontmatter and Lane
 374: note — every other surface points at the agent files. Trust is per-directory and interactive-only, so this lane runs in
 375: the main checkout this cycle, with the plan's Task 6 trusted scratch repo
 376: as the sole live-verification exception — a worktree trust story is
 377: future work.
 378: ````

 387: <!-- shared-contract:start -->
 388: ## The contract

 135: ## Design
 136: 
 137: ### 1. Agent: `agents/flash-implementer.md`
 138: 
 139: `model: haiku` frontmatter (wrapper/supervisor) plus an explicit `tools:`
 140: allowlist — Read, Grep, Glob, Bash; **no Edit, no Write, no NotebookEdit** —
 141: so the ergonomic path for the wrapper to type code itself does not exist.
 142: (Bash remains a write primitive in principle; the transcript/tree corroboration in
 143: §2 is the control that catches that path.) If the long-brief probe (§2)
 144: forces brief-via-file, the brief is written with a Bash heredoc — Write
 145: stays out of the allowlist.
 146: 
 147: Same input contract as `implementer.md` (ONE task's verbatim text + the
 148: plan's Global Constraints), same INPUT GAP rule. Report format gains one
 149: lane-specific line:
 150: 
 151: - **STATUS:** done | blocked | INPUT GAP: <what is missing>
 152: - **ROUTE:** resolved model ID as requested-and-propagated, from the log,
 153:   plus the retained log file's path AND the brain transcript's path
 154: - **FILES CHANGED:** actual paths from `git status` — on `blocked` this
 155:   MUST still list every path Flash already touched, so the session can
 156:   revert a partial write
 157: - **VERIFICATION:** each command run by the wrapper itself, with real output
 158: - **DEVIATIONS:** must be "none" or an explanation of why the task could
 159:   not be built as written
 160: 
 161: The parity test (§6) pins the shared sections (INPUT GAP rule, report
 162: headings other than ROUTE) byte-identical across both agent files; ROUTE is
 163: declared lane-specific and excluded from parity.
 164: 
 165: ### 2. Dispatch recipe
 166: 
 167: - Preflight, all blocking, before any dispatch:
 168:   1. `agy models` output contains `gemini-3.6-flash-medium` (also proves
 169:      sign-in and binary presence);
 170:   2. `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json` `trustedWorkspaces` contains
 171:      the workdir — if not, block with the exact re-trust instruction (one
 172:      interactive `agy` session in the workdir);
 173:   3. the same settings file carries NO file-writing per-tool allow rule
 174:      AT ALL (any `write_file(` entry, whatever path it names) — the
 175:      durable, call-site-invisible bypass class; this settings assertion is
 176:      the LOAD-BEARING permission control (a flag is at least visible
 177:      per-dispatch; a settings rule survives invisibly forever — tonight's
 178:      probing itself left one behind). Conservative by amendment (Sol
 179:      check-off F4): agy's path normalization is not offline-verifiable
 180:      (trust entries and allow rules already disagree on drive-letter
 181:      spelling on this machine), so the lane bans the rule CLASS rather
 182:      than attempting workdir path-matching;
 183:   4. `git status --porcelain` is EMPTY before dispatch (Sol check-off F2:
 184:      without a pre-dispatch baseline, pre-existing changes are
 185:      unattributable — a dirty tree is blocked, not absorbed).
 186: - Dispatch: `agy -p <brief> --model gemini-3.6-flash-medium
 187:   --add-dir <workdir> --log-file <fresh per-task log>`. Fresh log file per
 188:   attempt, written under the session scratchpad (never the repo tree — no
 189:   .gitignore dependency); each task's report carries its log path, and the
 190:   SDD execution record (the `.superpowers/sdd/<date>-<topic>/` workspace
 191:   the subagent-driven-development skill maintains) records per-task lane
 192:   provenance, which the diff-debate brief then carries. Adding an
 193:   implementer line to the frozen-plan debate record's Participants block is
 194:   declared scope for the plan debate to settle.
 195: - Brief-file lifecycle (Sol check-off F2): the brief is the SOLE transient
 196:   file the wrapper may create in the repo tree — a declared exception to
 197:   its never-write rule. Unique reserved name (`AGY-TASK-BRIEF-<unique>.md`,
 198:   unique suffix from the dispatch's log-file basename); collision
 199:   preflight (any existing `AGY-TASK-BRIEF-*` file in the workspace =
 200:   stale state = blocked); deletion guaranteed after agy exits — success,

 succeeded in 494ms:
 201:   failure, or interruption path alike — and always BEFORE any evidence
 202:   check, so the brief never appears in the attribution set.
 203: - Route check, every run, on the log file:
 204:   1. `Print mode: starting` line present with `model="gemini-3.6-flash-medium"`
 205:      (the RESOLVED ID is the comparand — same string §5 pins as canonical);
 206:   2. `Propagating selected model override` line present (presence-only; its
 207:      display label is a second namespace and is NOT matched);
 208:   3. transcript/tree corroboration (evidence source corrected by Sol
 209:      check-off F1 — the `--log-file` log carries NO file actions, probed):
 210:      parse `conversationID="<uuid>"` from the log's `Print mode: starting`
 211:      line, read the brain transcript at
 212:      `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/brain/<conversationID>/.system_generated/logs/transcript_full.jsonl`,
 213:      and require every path `git status` reports changed to appear in that
 214:      transcript as a successful file-changing action. A changed file the
 215:      transcript never mentions means the wrapper (or something else) typed
 216:      it — blocked regardless of test results. A missing transcript is
 217:      blocked. The ROUTE report line carries the transcript path beside the
 218:      log path.
 219:   This evidence class is CLIENT-side: the check verifies the route was
 220:   **requested and propagated**, never "used and confirmed" (the doctor

 222:   substitution is undetectable with today's evidence — named in Risks.

   7: 
   8: # Flash implementer (agy wrapper)
   9: 
  10: You supervise ONE task from a frozen implementation plan. Gemini 3.6 Flash
  11: does ALL the typing through the Antigravity CLI (`agy`); you do preflight,
  12: dispatch, evidence checks, verification, and honest reporting. You never
  13: edit or create repo files yourself — your tool grant has no Edit or Write,
  14: and using Bash to write repo content is equally forbidden: a changed file
  15: the brain transcript cannot account for fails the task. The one declared
  16: carve-out is the brief file below — the sole transient exception to the
  17: never-write rule, and it never survives to the evidence checks.
  18: 
  19: <!-- shared-contract:start -->
  20: ## The contract
  21: 
  22: - Build exactly what the task says: the files it lists, the code it shows,
  23:   the commands it specifies. Nothing else.
  24: - No improvements, no drive-by refactors, no added error handling, no scope
  25:   adjustments. A deviation is a defect even when it looks better — the diff
  26:   gets checked against the plan afterward, and unexplained drift fails it.
  27: - **INPUT GAP rule:** if the task references a file, interface, value, or
  28:   convention that is not in your brief and not discoverable at the exact
  29:   path the task names, STOP and report the gap. Never invent or guess the
  30:   missing piece.
  31: - Run the task's verification commands yourself and read the output. Never
  32:   claim completion without re-running verification — "should work" means
  33:   the task is not done.
  34: <!-- shared-contract:end -->
  35: 
  36: ## Inputs (from the dispatching controller)
  37: 
  38: - The task's verbatim text and the plan's Global Constraints.
  39: - The workspace directory (this cycle: the main checkout only, with the
  40:   sole live-verification exception — the plan's Task 6 trusted scratch
  41:   repo).
  42: - A log-file path OUTSIDE the workspace (the controller owns it; you never
  43:   place logs in the repo tree).
  44: 
  45: ## Preflight (all five must pass BEFORE dispatch)
  46: 
  47: 1. `agy models` (binary at `$LOCALAPPDATA/agy/bin/agy.exe`) — output must
  48:    contain `gemini-3.6-flash-medium`. Anything else (missing binary,
  49:    sign-out, missing model) is blocked.
  50: 2. `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json` — `trustedWorkspaces` must
  51:    contain the workspace directory. If not: blocked, and the report quotes
  52:    the fix ("run one interactive `agy` session in the workspace and approve
  53:    trust").
  54: 3. The same settings file must carry NO file-writing per-tool allow rule
  55:    at all — any `write_file(` entry, whatever path it names, is blocking.
  56:    A persisted settings allow rule is the durable, call-site-invisible
  57:    bypass class — its absence is the load-bearing permission control; path
  58:    spellings vary, so the rule CLASS is banned rather than path-matched.
  59:    If present: blocked, quoting the rule.
  60: 4. `git status --porcelain` in the workspace must be EMPTY. A dirty tree
  61:    makes authorship attribution impossible — blocked, quoting the paths.
  62: 5. No file matching `AGY-TASK-BRIEF-*` exists in the workspace — a stale
  63:    brief means an earlier dispatch died mid-cleanup: blocked.
  64: 
  65: ## Dispatch
  66: 
  67: 1. Write the brief to `<workspace>/AGY-TASK-BRIEF-<unique>.md` with a Bash
  68:    heredoc — `<unique>` is the dispatch log file's basename, so briefs
  69:    never collide. Content: the task's verbatim text, the Global
  70:    Constraints, and the exact files list. (stdin does not reach the model
  71:    in print mode — probed 2026-07-25; the workspace brief file is the
  72:    delivery mechanism.) This file is the sole transient exception to your
  73:    never-write rule.
  74: 2. Run (single line):
  75:    `agy -p "Read the file AGY-TASK-BRIEF-<unique>.md in the workspace and execute it exactly." --model gemini-3.6-flash-medium --add-dir <workspace> --log-file <log-path>`
  76: 3. Delete the brief file immediately after agy exits — on success, failure, and interruption alike — and always BEFORE any evidence check, so it never appears in `git status`. If your run is resumed after an interruption, delete any leftover brief FIRST.
  77: 
  78: ## Route and authorship checks (every run)
  79: 
  80: - On the log file: `Print mode: starting` line present containing
  81:   `model="gemini-3.6-flash-medium"`.
  82: - On the log file: `Propagating selected model override` line present
  83:   (presence only — its display label is not matched).
  84: - Transcript/tree corroboration: parse `conversationID="<uuid>"` from the
  85:   log's `Print mode: starting` line, then read the brain transcript at
  86:   `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/brain/<conversationID>/.system_generated/logs/transcript_full.jsonl`
  87:   (the `--log-file` log itself carries NO file actions — probed). Every path git status reports changed must appear in the brain transcript as a successful file-changing action. A changed file the transcript never
  88:   mentions means someone other than Flash typed it — blocked, no matter
  89:   what the tests say. A missing transcript is blocked.
  90: - This evidence is client-side: report the route as **requested and
  91:   propagated**, never "used and confirmed". Server-side substitution is
  92:   not detectable from this evidence class.
  93: 
  94: ## Failure handling — loud, never silent
  95: 
  96: Blocked (quote the exact output) on: any preflight failure, the print-mode
  97: soft-deny line ("auto-denied"), nonzero exit, a missing or mismatched
  98: route line, a corroboration mismatch, or writes diverted to agy's internal
  99: scratch (expected files absent from the tree). Never retry with

diff --git a/README.md b/README.md

@@ -46,41 +46,42 @@ The debate rules that keep this honest
   probe). Uncited claims are struck, not debated.
 - **Equal weight** — disagreements resolve by evidence, never by which model
   said it. Unresolvable points escalate to the user with both positions.
 - **Round cap** — 4 exchanges by default; convergence in one round on a
   sound plan is the system working, and manufactured objections are a
   protocol violation, not diligence.
 - **Session continuity** — the reviewer keeps debate state across rounds via
   `codex exec … resume <SESSION_ID>`; the full context is sent once.
 - **Final adjudication** — the session always has the last step: it
   verifies the reviewer's final round against the repo and emits the
   terminal verdict itself. A reviewer PASS/FIX is input, never the
   decision; genuine deadlocks escalate to the user. The rule attaches to
   the *role*, not the model — it holds whoever fills the session seat.

 |---|---|
 | `skills/multi-model-verify/` | The debate skill: both modes, debate protocol, frozen-plan format, model prompting notes, fallbacks/consent gate |

+| `agents/flash-implementer.md` | Zero-judgment Flash lane: haiku wrapper drives Gemini Flash through the Antigravity CLI headlessly; route + authorship evidence checked every run (model literal pinned in the file) |
 | `commands/drift-triage.md` | `/parallax:drift-triage` — reads the newest drift report, verifies each finding against the live contract surfaces, repairs on a branch |
 | `commands/doctor.md` | `/parallax:doctor` — operational health check: checkout-vs-installed version, hook registration, superpowers fingerprint, codex transport round-trip, drift task + pending entries. Reports, never fixes |

 ## Fails loud, never silent
 
 The governing rule
 (`skills/multi-model-verify/references/fallbacks.md`): **no transition that reduces
 vendor diversity, evidence quality, or conversation continuity happens
 without explicit user consent.**
 
 ```mermaid
 flowchart TD
     X[codex failure] --> R{one same-parameters retry}
     R -->|recovered| OK[debate continues]
     R -->|still failing| G["CONSENT GATE banner:<br/>what failed · what degraded mode<br/>would and would NOT verify"]
@@ -112,46 +113,45 @@ flowchart TD
   back to the config default, probed 2026-07-24.) The
   call itself runs with reroute-capable env vars stripped
   (`CODEX_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_BASE_URL`, `CODEX_HOME`) after a
   `codex login status` preflight that requires the first-party ChatGPT
   state, not merely exit 0.
 - A DEGRADED-frozen plan cannot produce an ordinary diff PASS: mode `diff`
   must first retrospectively cross-verify the plan's claims, and if
   cross-vendor is *still* unavailable the only terminal state is
   `ESCALATE — CROSS-VENDOR GATE UNSATISFIED`.
 - Missing reference material for a port is a **hard stop** (ask the user),
   never a degraded mode — a debate about remembered code is two models
   fabricating at each other.

   model supervises, delegates the body of work to the vendor CLI, and

 - **Cross-vendor reviewer** — a ONE-LINE swap: the canonical model id and
   reasoning effort live solely in
   `skills/multi-model-verify/references/model-prompting-notes.md`. The
   behavioral runner and the drift watch parse those declarations at
   runtime (failing loud if they vanish), and every instruction surface
   (SKILL.md transport commands, doctor, drift-triage) reads them from
   there; a consistency test forbids a hardcoded `-m` literal anywhere
   else.
 - **Session** — whatever model runs the session; the debate rules and
   final adjudication follow the seat automatically.

+---
+name: flash-implementer
+description: Zero-judgment Flash implementer for frozen-plan tasks. Use when executing build tasks from a debate-frozen implementation plan - give it ONE task's verbatim text plus the plan's Global Constraints and a log-file path. It delegates ALL code-writing to Gemini 3.6 Flash via the Antigravity CLI headlessly, verifies route and authorship evidence, runs the task's verification itself, and reports. It never types repo code and never makes design decisions.
+model: haiku
+tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash
+---
+
+# Flash implementer (agy wrapper)
+
+You supervise ONE task from a frozen implementation plan. Gemini 3.6 Flash
+does ALL the typing through the Antigravity CLI (`agy`); you do preflight,
+dispatch, evidence checks, verification, and honest reporting. You never
+edit or create repo files yourself — your tool grant has no Edit or Write,
+and using Bash to write repo content is equally forbidden: a changed file
+the brain transcript cannot account for fails the task. The one declared
+carve-out is the brief file below — the sole transient exception to the
+never-write rule, and it never survives to the evidence checks.
+
+<!-- shared-contract:start -->
+## The contract
+
+- Build exactly what the task says: the files it lists, the code it shows,
+  the commands it specifies. Nothing else.
+- No improvements, no drive-by refactors, no added error handling, no scope
+  adjustments. A deviation is a defect even when it looks better — the diff
+  gets checked against the plan afterward, and unexplained drift fails it.
+- **INPUT GAP rule:** if the task references a file, interface, value, or
+  convention that is not in your brief and not discoverable at the exact
+  path the task names, STOP and report the gap. Never invent or guess the
+  missing piece.
+- Run the task's verification commands yourself and read the output. Never
+  claim completion without re-running verification — "should work" means
+  the task is not done.
+<!-- shared-contract:end -->
+
+## Inputs (from the dispatching controller)
+
+- The task's verbatim text and the plan's Global Constraints.
+- The workspace directory (this cycle: the main checkout only, with the
+  sole live-verification exception — the plan's Task 6 trusted scratch
+  repo).
+- A log-file path OUTSIDE the workspace (the controller owns it; you never
+  place logs in the repo tree).
+
+## Preflight (all five must pass BEFORE dispatch)
+
+1. `agy models` (binary at `$LOCALAPPDATA/agy/bin/agy.exe`) — output must
+   contain `gemini-3.6-flash-medium`. Anything else (missing binary,
+   sign-out, missing model) is blocked.
+2. `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json` — `trustedWorkspaces` must
+   contain the workspace directory. If not: blocked, and the report quotes
+   the fix ("run one interactive `agy` session in the workspace and approve
+   trust").
+3. The same settings file must carry NO file-writing per-tool allow rule
+   at all — any `write_file(` entry, whatever path it names, is blocking.
+   A persisted settings allow rule is the durable, call-site-invisible
+   bypass class — its absence is the load-bearing permission control; path
+   spellings vary, so the rule CLASS is banned rather than path-matched.
+   If present: blocked, quoting the rule.
+4. `git status --porcelain` in the workspace must be EMPTY. A dirty tree
+   makes authorship attribution impossible — blocked, quoting the paths.
+5. No file matching `AGY-TASK-BRIEF-*` exists in the workspace — a stale
+   brief means an earlier dispatch died mid-cleanup: blocked.
+
+## Dispatch
+
+1. Write the brief to `<workspace>/AGY-TASK-BRIEF-<unique>.md` with a Bash
+   heredoc — `<unique>` is the dispatch log file's basename, so briefs
+   never collide. Content: the task's verbatim text, the Global
+   Constraints, and the exact files list. (stdin does not reach the model
+   in print mode — probed 2026-07-25; the workspace brief file is the
+   delivery mechanism.) This file is the sole transient exception to your
+   never-write rule.
+2. Run (single line):
+   `agy -p "Read the file AGY-TASK-BRIEF-<unique>.md in the workspace and execute it exactly." --model gemini-3.6-flash-medium --add-dir <workspace> --log-file <log-path>`
+3. Delete the brief file immediately after agy exits — on success, failure, and interruption alike — and always BEFORE any evidence check, so it never appears in `git status`. If your run is resumed after an interruption, delete any leftover brief FIRST.
+
+## Route and authorship checks (every run)
+
+- On the log file: `Print mode: starting` line present containing
+  `model="gemini-3.6-flash-medium"`.
+- On the log file: `Propagating selected model override` line present
+  (presence only — its display label is not matched).
+- Transcript/tree corroboration: parse `conversationID="<uuid>"` from the
+  log's `Print mode: starting` line, then read the brain transcript at
+  `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/brain/<conversationID>/.system_generated/logs/transcript_full.jsonl`
+  (the `--log-file` log itself carries NO file actions — probed). Every path git status reports changed must appear in the brain transcript as a successful file-changing action. A changed file the transcript never
+  mentions means someone other than Flash typed it — blocked, no matter
+  what the tests say. A missing transcript is blocked.
+- This evidence is client-side: report the route as **requested and
+  propagated**, never "used and confirmed". Server-side substitution is
+  not detectable from this evidence class.
+
+## Failure handling — loud, never silent
+
+Blocked (quote the exact output) on: any preflight failure, the print-mode
+soft-deny line ("auto-denied"), nonzero exit, a missing or mismatched
+route line, a corroboration mismatch, or writes diverted to agy's internal
+scratch (expected files absent from the tree). Never retry with
+`--dangerously-skip-permissions` — that flag is forbidden in this lane, as
+is ANY approval-bypass flag or persisted per-tool allow rule added to agy
+settings. Never complete the work yourself: rerouting a blocked task to a
+Claude tier is the user's decision, recorded in the plan's Escalated
+points — not yours.
+
+## Report format (your final message)
+
+- **STATUS:** done | blocked | INPUT GAP: <exactly what is missing>
+- **ROUTE:** the resolved model ID as requested and propagated, plus the
+  retained log file's path AND the brain transcript's path
+- **FILES CHANGED:** actual paths from `git status` — on blocked, STILL
+  list every path Flash already touched so the session can revert a
+  partial write
+- **VERIFICATION:** each command you ran yourself, with its real output
+  (condensed)
+- **DEVIATIONS:** must be "none" — anything else means you stopped and are
+  explaining why the task could not be built as written
+
+## Lane note
+
+This agent pins the Flash implementation lane. Canonical model literal:
+`gemini-3.6-flash-medium` (Gemini 3.6 Flash, medium reasoning effort,
+Antigravity CLI resolved ID). The literal lives ONLY here;
+`implementer.md` pins its own lane's model in its frontmatter and Lane
+note — every other surface points at the agent files. Trust is per-directory and interactive-only, so this lane runs in
+the main checkout this cycle, with the plan's Task 6 trusted scratch repo
+as the sole live-verification exception — a worktree trust story is future work.

+"""Contract pins for the Flash implementer lane (agents/flash-implementer.md).
+
+Amended by design spec 2026-07-25 (advisory review B1-B8): these tests pin
+the agent file's contract text so drift in the dispatch recipe, route
+check, forbidden-bypass class, or report format fails offline with zero
+CLI calls. The two agent files are the only allowed homes for the
+implementer model literal.
+"""
+import re
+from pathlib import Path
+
+REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
+FLASH = REPO / "agents" / "flash-implementer.md"
+CLASSIC = REPO / "agents" / "implementer.md"
+CANONICAL_ID = "gemini-3.6-flash-medium"
+SHARED_START = "<!-- shared-contract:start -->"
+SHARED_END = "<!-- shared-contract:end -->"
+
+
+def _read(p):
+    return p.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
+
+
+def _frontmatter(text):
+    m = re.match(r"^---\n(.*?)\n---\n", text, re.DOTALL)
+    assert m, "missing frontmatter"
+    return m.group(1)
+
+
+def test_flash_frontmatter_pins_model_and_tools():
+    fm = _frontmatter(_read(FLASH))
+    assert re.search(r"^model: haiku$", fm, re.MULTILINE)
+    m = re.search(r"^tools: (.+)$", fm, re.MULTILINE)
+    assert m, "tools allowlist missing"
+    tools = [t.strip() for t in m.group(1).split(",")]
+    assert sorted(tools) == ["Bash", "Glob", "Grep", "Read"]
+
+
+def test_flash_dispatch_contract():
+    body = _read(FLASH)
+    assert "--model " + CANONICAL_ID in body
+    assert "--add-dir" in body
+    assert "--log-file" in body
+    # unique-suffix brief name + full lifecycle, pinned by exact sentence
+    # fragments so a regression cannot pass on loose keywords
+    # (Sol check-off round 2, finding 3)
+    assert "AGY-TASK-BRIEF-" in body
+    assert "sole transient exception" in body.lower()
+    assert "the dispatch log file's basename" in body
+    assert "on success, failure, and interruption alike" in body
+    # stdin is probed-dead in print mode; the body must not suggest it
+    assert "stdin" not in body.lower() or "does not reach" in body.lower()
+
+
+def test_flash_route_check_strings():
+    body = _read(FLASH)
+    assert 'Print mode: starting' in body
+    assert 'model="' + CANONICAL_ID + '"' in body
+    assert "Propagating selected model override" in body
+    assert "requested and propagated" in body
+    assert "used and confirmed" not in body.replace(
+        'never "used and confirmed"', "")
+    # transcript/tree corroboration (Sol check-off F1: the log carries no
+    # file actions; evidence lives in the brain transcript)
+    assert "transcript_full.jsonl" in body

+        assert heading in classic, heading

+def _shared_block(text, path):
+    assert SHARED_START in text and SHARED_END in text, (
+        "missing shared-contract markers in " + str(path))
+    return text.split(SHARED_START)[1].split(SHARED_END)[0]

 # Frozen plan format
 
 The debate's output is a superpowers-compatible implementation plan. The
-implementer (Sonnet 5 or the session model, via superpowers
+implementer (the pinned lane in `agents/`, or the session model, via superpowers
 subagent-driven-development or executing-plans) follows it with **zero
 judgment calls** — anything the plan leaves open is a plan defect, found in
 mode `diff` as drift.
 
 ## Base format
 
 Follow the superpowers writing-plans template exactly (header with Goal /
 Architecture / Tech Stack / Global Constraints; bite-sized checkbox tasks
 with exact Files / Interfaces / complete code / exact commands / expected
 output; no placeholders). Every task's verification command must be able
 to FAIL: the debate checks each one for oracle adequacy — a proof that
 would pass while the feature is broken (a compile check standing in for a
 behavior check, a test that never exercises the changed path) is a plan
 defect, found in the debate, not in production. Save location: the
 superpowers default
 `docs/superpowers/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<feature-name>.md`, unless the project
 overrides it (example: KitnEssentials uses `dev/docs/superpowers/plans/`).

 port (KitnDev-family example — adapt the specifics per project):

  61: 
  62: | Piece | What it does |
  63: |---|---|

  65: | `hooks/` | PostToolUse + PostToolUseFailure hook (matcher `Task\|Agent`): fingerprints the superpowers code-reviewer dispatch, injects the mode-`diff` reminder with matching SHAs; inert everywhere else |

  76: ## Fails loud, never silent
  77: 
  78: The governing rule
  79: (`skills/multi-model-verify/references/fallbacks.md`): **no transition that reduces
  80: vendor diversity, evidence quality, or conversation continuity happens
  81: without explicit user consent.**
  82: 
  83: ```mermaid
  84: flowchart TD
  85:     X[codex failure] --> R{one same-parameters retry}
  86:     R -->|recovered| OK[debate continues]
  87:     R -->|still failing| G["CONSENT GATE banner:<br/>what failed · what degraded mode<br/>would and would NOT verify"]
  88:     X -. "quota-exhausted:<br/>skip the retry" .-> G
  89:     X -. "missing-rollout resume:<br/>skip the retry" .-> G
  90:     G -->|fix codex| OK
  91:     G -->|run degraded| D["single-vendor, visibly flagged;<br/>DEGRADED plan poisons any diff PASS"]
  92:     G -->|abort or unattended| B2[BLOCKED / DEGRADED-NOT-AUTHORIZED]
  93: ```
  94: 
  95: - One same-parameters retry is the **only** automatic recovery; every other
  96:   transition stops at the consent gate. Unattended runs fail closed.
  97:   (Pre-dispatch failures retry free; a post-dispatch retry is the bounded
  98:   re-spend — never a loop.)
  99: - Session/weekly usage limits get a dedicated `quota-exhausted` class: no
 100:   retry (quota windows don't clear in seconds), and the banner quotes
 101:   codex's reset time verbatim. A resume failing with the missing-rollout
 102:   signature ("no rollout found ... code -32600", probed 2026-07-24) also
 103:   skips the retry — the rollout is gone; straight to the session-loss
 104:   consent gate.
 105: - **Effective-route check (0.6.0; `sandbox:` line added in 0.8.0)**: every
 106:   codex call's startup header
 107:   (`model:` / `provider:` / `reasoning effort:` / `sandbox: read-only`,
 108:   plus the `session id:` on
 109:   resume) is verified against the canonical declarations — a config.toml
 110:   override or profile silently swapping the reviewer is a named
 111:   `route-mismatch` class: no retry, reply discarded, consent gate.
 112:   (Sandbox mode has no continuity across resumes — an omitted flag falls
 113:   back to the config default, probed 2026-07-24.) The
 114:   call itself runs with reroute-capable env vars stripped
 115:   (`CODEX_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_BASE_URL`, `CODEX_HOME`) after a
 116:   `codex login status` preflight that requires the first-party ChatGPT
 117:   state, not merely exit 0.
 118: - A DEGRADED-frozen plan cannot produce an ordinary diff PASS: mode `diff`
 119:   must first retrospectively cross-verify the plan's claims, and if
 120:   cross-vendor is *still* unavailable the only terminal state is
 121:   `ESCALATE — CROSS-VENDOR GATE UNSATISFIED`.
 122: - Missing reference material for a port is a **hard stop** (ask the user),
 123:   never a degraded mode — a debate about remembered code is two models
 124:   fabricating at each other.
 125: 
 126: ## Swapping lanes
 127: 
 128: Every role is a plug — the contracts attach to roles, not models. Today's
 129: lineup (session / cross-vendor reviewer / cheap implementer) is one
 130: configuration:
 131: 
 132: - **Implementer, Claude tier** — edit one line: `model:` in
 133:   `agents/implementer.md` frontmatter (any Claude tier is a drop-in). The contract (zero judgment calls, INPUT GAP rule, structured
 134:   report) stays identical whoever fills it.
 135: - **Implementer, cross-vendor** (the fable-advisor v3 Grok pattern) —
 136:   agent frontmatter only accepts Claude tiers, so a vendor swap uses the
 137:   supervisor pattern `agents/flash-implementer.md` implements (documented in `agents/implementer.md`'s Lane note): a cheap Claude
 138:   model supervises, delegates the body of work to the vendor CLI, and
 139:   re-runs verification itself.
 140: - **Cross-vendor reviewer** — a ONE-LINE swap: the canonical model id and
 141:   reasoning effort live solely in
 142:   `skills/multi-model-verify/references/model-prompting-notes.md`. The
 143:   behavioral runner and the drift watch parse those declarations at
 144:   runtime (failing loud if they vanish), and every instruction surface
 145:   (SKILL.md transport commands, doctor, drift-triage) reads them from
 170: checkout edits are NOT live until re-synced:
 171: 
 172: ```
 173: claude plugin marketplace add <path-to-this-checkout>
 174: claude plugin install parallax@parallax
 175: # after edits: bump .claude-plugin/plugin.json version, then
 176: claude plugin update parallax@parallax   # qualified name required
 177: # restart the Claude Code session to re-register hooks/skills
 178: ```
 179: 
 180: Forgetting a step here is the failure mode that looks like a plugin bug: a
 181: stale cache runs yesterday's skill, a missed restart leaves the hook
 182: unregistered. `/parallax:doctor` reports both, plus the fingerprint, the
 183: codex transport, quota headroom (best-effort, experimental), and any
 184: unresolved drift — in one table.
 185:

   1: # Frozen plan format
   2: 
   3: The debate's output is a superpowers-compatible implementation plan. The
   4: implementer (the pinned lane in `agents/`, or the session model, via superpowers
   5: subagent-driven-development or executing-plans) follows it with **zero
   6: judgment calls** — anything the plan leaves open is a plan defect, found in
   7: mode `diff` as drift.
   8: 
   9: ## Base format
  10: 
  11: Follow the superpowers writing-plans template exactly (header with Goal /
  12: Architecture / Tech Stack / Global Constraints; bite-sized checkbox tasks
  13: with exact Files / Interfaces / complete code / exact commands / expected
  14: output; no placeholders). Every task's verification command must be able
  15: to FAIL: the debate checks each one for oracle adequacy — a proof that
  16: would pass while the feature is broken (a compile check standing in for a
  17: behavior check, a test that never exercises the changed path) is a plan
  18: defect, found in the debate, not in production. Save location: the
  19: superpowers default
  20: `docs/superpowers/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<feature-name>.md`, unless the project
  21: overrides it (example: KitnEssentials uses `dev/docs/superpowers/plans/`).
  22: 
  23: Port-specific Global Constraints to copy in verbatim when the work is a
  24: port (KitnDev-family example — adapt the specifics per project):
  25: 
  26: - Project naming/style conventions the port must follow (KE example:
  27:   KE namespace, flat DB keys, `KE:ApplyFontToText` for SOFTOUTLINE-capable
  28:   text, string literals only — see the module-port skill).
  29: - Reference source and version the port tracks:
  30:   `References/<name>/` at the fidelity the debate agreed on (faithful core
  31:   logic; project-specific deviations listed one per line WITH the debate
  32:   round that approved each).
  33: - Platform/API constraints the debate verified, each with its citation
  34:   (KE example: WoW 12.0 secret-value rules).
  35: 
  36: ## Debate record appendix (REQUIRED)
  37: 
  38: Every frozen plan ends with:
  39: 
  40: ```markdown
  41: ---
  42: 
  43: ## Debate record
  44: 
  45: **Participants:** <session model> (session) / <reviewer model> (codex exec, session <id>)
  46: **Rounds used:** N of CAP
  47: **Outcome:** converged | converged with amendments | escalated
  48: **Verification status:** FULL | DEGRADED
  49: **Degradation:** none | codex-missing | model-rejected | auth-expired | quota-exhausted | continuity-lost | <class>
  50: **Authorized by:** n/a | user at round N | not-authorized
  51: **Raw rounds:** <paths to the verbatim round replies/transcripts> | not retained
  52: 
  53: ### Resolved points
  54: | # | Claim | Raised by | Outcome | Evidence |
  55: |---|-------|-----------|---------|----------|
  56: | 1 | ...   | reviewer  | accepted into Task 3 | References/<addon>/<file>:<line> |
  57: | 2 | ...   | session   | refuted  | .wow-api-reference/<file> |
  58: | 3 | ...   | reviewer  | struck (no citation) | — |
  59: 
  60: ### Escalated points (user-decided)
  61: | # | Question | Session position | Reviewer position | Owner's call |
  62: |---|----------|------------------|-------------------|--------------|
  63: 
  64: ### Degraded-mode note
  65: (required whenever Verification status is DEGRADED — name what was skipped,
  66: why, and the recorded user authorization. The Participants line must name
  67: the actual participants, never the placeholder template line.)
  68: ```
  69: 
  70: The three status fields are structured on purpose: mode `diff` parses
  71: `Verification status` to enforce the degraded-plan poisoning rule (see
  72: SKILL.md) — prose notes cannot be enforced. `Raw rounds` records where the
  73: verbatim reviewer replies live (scratchpad transcripts are temporary — if
  74: they were not copied somewhere durable, say `not retained`): the summary
  75: tables above are the adjudication, not the provenance, and a later dispute
  76: about what the reviewer actually said needs the raw text or an honest
  77: "gone".
  78: 
  79: The appendix is the audit trail: mode `diff` re-reads it to know which
  80: deviations were approved and which claims were struck. A frozen plan without
  81: the appendix was not verified — treat it as an unfrozen draft.
  82: 
  83: ## Freezing
  84: 
  85: A plan is frozen when: both sides verdict PASS — or the final round's FIXes

def _grading_view(entry):
    """A case entry minus selection metadata: `surface` says WHICH files

def changed_paths_vs(base):
    """Committed + uncommitted (working tree vs base) plus untracked."""
    return (_git_lines("diff", "--name-only", base)
            + _git_lines("ls-files", "--others", "--exclude-standard"))
